</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Idiopathic generalized epilepsy &#x00026; myoclonic epilepsy (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/607682" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">607682</a>) &#x00026; episodic ataxia type 5 (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/613855" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">613855</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Early-onset epileptic disorder that is often assoc w/myoclonic, generalized tonic-clonic, or absence seizures w/photosensitivity reported in some persons&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>CERS1</i>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/epm1/">
<i>CSTB</i>
</a>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/lafora/">
<i>EPM2A</i>
</a>
<br />
<i>GOSR2</i>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/kcnc1-dis/">
<i>KCNC1</i>
</a>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/kctd7-epilepsy/">
<i>KCTD7</i>
</a>
<br />
<i>LMNB2</i>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/lafora/">
<i>NHLRC1</i>
</a>
<br />
<i>PRDM8</i>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/me-ataxia/">
<i>PRICKLE1</i>
</a>
<br />
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/amrf/">
<i>SCARB2</i>
</a>
<br />
<i>SEMA6B</i>
<br />
<i>SLC7A6OS</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Myoclonic epilepsy (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/phenotypicSeries/PS254800" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">PS254800</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR<br />AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Can cause dementia or pseudodementia because of unrelenting seizures &#x00026; anticonvulsant side effects</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>CLN5</i>
<br />
<i>CLN6</i>
<br />
<i>CLN8</i>
<br />
<i>PPT1</i>
<br />
<i>TPP1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">CLN1 disease &#x00026; CLN2 disease (<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/ncl/">neuronal ceroid lipofuscinoses</a> [NCLs])</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Phenotypes incl in NCLs that overlap w/AHS are CLN1 disease, classic infantile (previously classic infantile NCL, INCL, Santavuori-Haltia) &#x00026; CLN2 disease, classic late infantile (previously late-infantile NCL, LINCL, Jansky-Bielschowsky disease)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>COQ8A</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>COQ8A</i>-related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/coq10-def/">primary coenzyme Q<sub>10</sub> (CoQ<sub>10</sub>) deficiency</a></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Epilepsia partialis continua has been described in some affected persons.&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Targeted therapy: Persons w/primary CoQ<sub>10</sub> deficiency may respond well to high-dose oral CoQ<sub>10</sub> supplementation.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>CTSA</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Galactosialidosis (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/256540" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">256540</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">During infancy &#x00026; early childhood, storage diseases can be assoc w/progressive encephalopathy w/primary involvement of cortical gray matter &#x00026; refractory epilepsy.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>DGUOK</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/dguok-mtddepl/">Deoxyguanosine kinase deficiency</a> (DGUOK deficiency)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In contrast to <i>POLG-</i>related AHS, DGUOK deficiency is not characterized by seizures or brain imaging abnormalities.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>DNA2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mtdna-md-ov/"><i>DNA2</i>-related mtDNA maintenance defect</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">PEO &#x00026; PEO w/systemic involvement&#x000a0;<sup>5</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>FBXL4</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/fbxl4-mtddepl/"><i>FBXL4</i>-related encephalomyopathic mtDNA depletion syndrome</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Early infantile encephalopathy, hypotonia, lactic acidosis, &#x00026; mtDNA depletion&#x000a0;<sup>6</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>FOLR1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/folr1-cft-def/"><i>FOLR1</i>-related cerebral folate transport deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neonatal-onset progressive encephalopathy w/refractory seizures</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Targeted therapy: Oral administration of 5-formylTHF is usually sufficient to bring CSF folate levels into normal range for age.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>HEXA</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/tay-sachs/">Hexosaminidase A deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="2" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">During infancy &#x00026; early childhood, storage diseases can be assoc w/progressive encephalopathy w/primary involvement of cortical gray matter &#x00026; refractory epilepsy.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>HEXB</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/sandhoff/">Sandhoff disease</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>MGME1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>MGME1-</i>related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mtdna-md-ov/">mtDNA maintenance defect</a></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Multisystemic mitochondrial <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> w/PEO, emaciation, &#x00026; respiratory failure</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>MPV17</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mpv17-mtdep/"><i>MPV17</i>-related mtDNA maintenance defect</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Multisystemic mitochondrial disease w/liver dysfunction, brain &#x00026; peripheral nerve disease, gastrointestinal dysmotility, &#x00026; lactic acidosis</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>MT-TK</i>&#x000a0;<sup>7</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/merff/">MERFF</a>&#x000a0;<sup>8</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See footnote 9.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Multisystemic mitochondrial disorder characterized by myoclonus (often 1st symptom) followed by generalized epilepsy, ataxia, weakness, exercise intolerance, &#x00026; dementia</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Onset can occur from childhood to adulthood, following normal early development</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Common findings are ptosis, hearing loss, short stature, optic atrophy, cardiomyopathy, cardiac dysrhythmias, &#x00026; peripheral neuropathy</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>MT-TL1</i>&#x000a0;<sup>10</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/melas/">MELAS</a>&#x000a0;<sup>8,&#x000a0;11</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See footnote 12.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Multisystemic mitochondrial disorder w/onset typically occurring in childhood</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Onset of symptoms is often ages 2-10 yrs.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Most common initial symptoms are generalized tonic-clonic seizures, recurrent headaches, anorexia, &#x00026; recurrent vomiting.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Seizures are often assoc w/stroke-like episodes of transient hemiparesis or cortical blindness. The cumulative residual effects of the stroke-like episodes gradually impair motor abilities, vision, &#x00026; mentation by adolescence or young adulthood.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sensorineural hearing loss is common.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>NDUFS4</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">NADH coenzyme Q reductase deficiency (OMIM <a href="https://www.omim.org/entry/252010" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">252010</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Epilepsia partialis continua has been described in some affected persons.&#x000a0;<sup>13</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>NEU1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Infantile sialidosis (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/256550" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">256550</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">During infancy &#x00026; early childhood, storage diseases can be assoc w/progressive encephalopathy w/primary involvement of cortical gray matter &#x00026; refractory epilepsy.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>OPA1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Optic atrophy type 1 (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/165500" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">165500</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Childhood-onset visual loss</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PABPN1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/opmd/">Oculopharyngeal muscular dystrophy</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Progressive adult-onset myopathy w/ ptosis, dysphagia, &#x00026; proximal weakness</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PLPBP</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/plpbp-def/">PLPBP deficiency</a> (pyridoxine-dependent epilepsy)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neonatal-onset progressive encephalopathy w/refractory seizures</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Targeted therapy: Pyridoxine is the first-line therapy. Most individuals have a favorable response to pyridoxine.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PNPO</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/pnpo-def/">PNPO deficiency</a> (pyridoxine-dependent epilepsy)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neonatal-onset progressive encephalopathy w/refractory seizures</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Targeted therapy: ~60% of persons are resistant to pyridoxine &#x00026; require treatment with pyridoxal 5'-phosphate; ~40% respond to pyridoxine alone.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>POLG2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mtdna-md-ov/"><i>POLG2</i>-related mtDNA maintenance defect</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Adult-onset PEO &#x00026; multisystemic mitochondrial disease w/mtDNA depletion</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>RNASEH1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>RNASEH1-</i>related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mtdna-md-ov/">mtDNA maintenance defect</a></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">PEO &#x00026; multisystemic mitochondrial disease w/mtDNA depletion</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>RRM2B</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/rrm2b-mtddepl/"><i>RRM2B</i> mtDNA maintenance defects</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR<br />AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neonatal-onset hypotonia, lactic acidosis, &#x00026; neurologic deterioration, w/ or w/o renal tubular dysfunction</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Adult-onset PEO, variable gastrointestinal dysmotility, multisystemic mitochondrial disease</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SCN1A</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/gefs/"><i>SCN1A</i> seizure disorders</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At the severe end of the spectrum, severe infantile epileptic disorder that appears progressive at onset but usually plateaus into developmental arrest/delay</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SCN2A</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>SCN2A-</i>related early infantile epileptic encephalopathy (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/613721" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">613721</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Severe infantile epileptic disorder that appears progressive at onset but usually plateaus into developmental arrest/delay</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SCO1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>SCO1-</i>related disorders (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/603644" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">603644</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Infantile-onset multisystemic mitochondrial disease, Leigh-like syndrome w/cardiac hypertrophy &#x00026; failure</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SLC25A19</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/amish-mcph/"><i>SLC25A19</i>-related thiamine metabolism dysfunction</a> (incl Amish lethal microcephaly &#x00026; thiamine metabolism dysfunction syndrome 4)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Targeted therapy: Oral thiamine treatment is critical from the time of diagnosis. This treatment is lifelong. It prevents metabolic decompensation &#x00026; improves outcomes.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SLC25A4</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>SLC25A4-</i>related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mtdna-md-ov/">mtDNA maintenance defect</a></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Primary presenting features are myopathy, hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, &#x00026; ophthalmoplegia.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SLC46A1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/folate-mal/">Hereditary folate malabsorption</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neonatal-onset progressive encephalopathy w/refractory seizures</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Targeted therapy: Early treatment w/oral 5-formylTHF or, preferably, the active isomer of 5-formylTHF (Isovorin<sup>&#x000ae;</sup> or Fusilev<sup>&#x000ae;</sup>) readily corrects the systemic folate deficiency &#x00026;, if the dose is sufficient, can achieve CSF folate levels that prevent or mitigate the neurologic consequences of hereditary folate malabsorption.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SUCLA2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/sucla2-def/"><i>SUCLA2</i>-related mtDNA depletion syndrome, encephalomyopathic form w/methylmalonic aciduria</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="2" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Infantile encephalomyopathy, methylmalonic aciduria, epilepsy, hepatopathy, &#x00026; cardiomyopathy</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SUCLG1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/suclg1-mtddepl/"><i>SUCLG1-</i>related mtDNA depletion syndrome, encephalomyopathic form w/methylmalonic aciduria</a>&#x000a0;<sup>14</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SUOX</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/iso-def/">Isolated sulfite oxidase deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neonatal-onset progressive encephalopathy w/refractory seizures</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Usually present earlier in infancy than AHS</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>TBC1D24</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/tbc1d24-dis/"><i>TBC1D24</i>-related disorders</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR&#x000a0;<sup>15</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Epilepsia partialis continua has been described in some affected persons.&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>TK2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/tk2-mtddepl/"><i>TK2</i>-related mtDNA maintenance defect, myopathic form</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At the severe end of the spectrum, infantile-onset myopathy w/neurologic involvement &#x00026; rapid progression to early death</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>TWNK</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Autosomal dominant PEO, infantile-onset spinocerebellar ataxia, <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/perrault/">Perrault syndrome</a>, &#x00026; <i>TWNK-</i>related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mtdna-md-ov/">mtDNA maintenance defect</a>&#x000a0;<sup>16</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR<br />AD</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Digenic inheritance of PEO has been reported in 2 persons w/<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/double-heterozygosity/">double heterozygosity</a> for a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> &#x00026; a <i>TWNK</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.&#x000a0;<sup>17</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>TYMP</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mngie/">Mitochondrial neurogastrointestinal encephalopathy</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Cachexia, gastrointestinal dysmotility, peripheral neuropathy, PEO, leukoencephalopathy&#x000a0;<sup>18</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Single large-scale mitochondrial DNA <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> ranging in size from 1.1 to 10 kb</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Chronic progressive external ophthalmoplegia (CPEO) &#x00026; Kearns-Sayre syndrome (KSS) (See <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/kss/">Single Large-Scale Mitochondrial DNA Deletion Syndromes</a>.)</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See footnote 19.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.genes_of_interest_in_the_differ_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>CPEO in a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/simplex/">simplex</a> case or when there is a maternal family history can be the result of a large-scale single <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> of mtDNA that may only be detected in limited tissues (e.g., skeletal muscle). CPEO is sometimes complicated by mild proximal muscle weakness &#x00026; dysphagia &#x00026; can be considered to lie on a spectrum of disease from pure CPEO to KSS.&#x000a0;<sup>20</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A multisystemic disorder defined by the triad of onset age &#x0003c;20 years, pigmentary retinopathy, &#x00026; PEO. In addition, persons have &#x02265;1 of the following: cardiac conduction block, CSF protein concentration &#x0003e;100 mg/dL, or cerebellar ataxia. Onset is usually in childhood. PEO, characterized by ptosis, paralysis of the extraocular muscles (ophthalmoplegia), &#x00026; variably severe proximal limb weakness, is relatively benign.&#x000a0;<sup>21</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">5-formytetrahydrofolate = 5-formylTHF; AD = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a>; AHS = Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome; AR = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a>; ASM = anti-seizure medication; CSF = cerebrospinal fluid; GRACILE syndrome = <i>g</i>rowth <i>r</i>estriction, <i>a</i>minoaciduria, <i>c</i>holestasis, <i>i</i>ron overload, <i>l</i>actic acidosis, and <i>e</i>arly death syndrome; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a>; PEO = progressive external ophthalmoplegia; XL = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/x-linked/">X-linked</a></p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.1"><p class="no_margin">Genes are ordered alphabetically.</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.mameni_kien_.2017.74">Mameni&#x00161;kien&#x00117; &#x00026; Wolf [2017]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.3"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.amadori.2022.104450">Amadori et al [2022]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.4"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.hikmat.2016.1188">Hikmat et al [2016]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.5"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.ronchi.2013.293">Ronchi et al [2013]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>6. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.6"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.gai.2013.482">Gai et al [2013]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>7. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.7"><p class="no_margin">The m.8344A&#x0003e;G <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in the mitochondrial <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> <i>MT-TK</i> is present in more than 80% of affected individuals with typical findings. Pathogenic variants in <i>MT-TF</i>, <i>MT-TH</i>, <i>MT-TI</i>, <i>MT-TL1</i>, <i>MT-TP</i>, <i>MT-TS1</i>, and <i>MT-TS2</i> have also been described in a subset of individuals with MERRF.</p></div></dd><dt>8. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.8"><p class="no_margin">Evidence to date suggests that diabetes and cardiomyopathy are not common in <i>POLG</i>-related disorders, distinguishing <i>POLG</i>-related disorders from other multisystemic mitochondrial diseases.</p></div></dd><dt>9. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.9"><p class="no_margin">MERRF is caused by pathogenic variants in mtDNA and is transmitted by maternal inheritance.</p></div></dd><dt>10. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.10"><p class="no_margin">The m.3243A&#x0003e;G <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in the mitochondrial <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> <i>MT-TL1</i> is present in approximately 80% of individuals with MELAS. Pathogenic variants in <i>MT-TL1</i> or other mtDNA genes, particularly <i>MT-ND5</i>, can also cause this disorder</p></div></dd><dt>11. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.11"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.hirano.1992.125">Hirano et al [1992]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>12. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.12"><p class="no_margin">MELAS is caused by pathogenic variants in mtDNA and is transmitted by maternal inheritance.</p></div></dd><dt>13. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.13"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.antozzi.1995.152">Antozzi et al [1995]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>14. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.14"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.molaei_ramsheh.2020.9699">Molaei Ramsheh et al [2020]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>15. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.15"><p class="no_margin">Most <i>TBC1D24</i>-related disorders are inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> manner.</p></div></dd><dt>16. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.16"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.peter.2020.408">Peter &#x00026; Falkenberg [2020]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>17. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.17"><p class="no_margin"><a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.van_goethem.2003a.175">Van Goethem et al [2003a]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.da_pozzo.2015.1713">Da Pozzo et al [2015]</a></p></div></dd><dt>18. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.18"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.pacitti.2018.669">Pacitti et al [2018]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>19. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.19"><p class="no_margin">SLSMDSs are almost never inherited, suggesting that these disorders are typically caused by a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> single large-scale mitochondrial DNA <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> (SLSMD) that occurs in the mother's oocytes during <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a> development or in the embryo during embryogenesis.</p></div></dd><dt>20. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.20"><p class="no_margin">Some individuals with CPEO (&#x0003c;20%) have a pathogenic <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/single-nucleotide-variant/">single-nucleotide variant</a> of mtDNA (e.g., m.3243A&#x0003e;G).</p></div></dd><dt>21. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.4.21"><p class="no_margin">Most individuals with KSS have a common <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> of 4,977 nucleotides involving 12 mitochondrial genes.</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><div id="alpers.Other_Disorders_to_Consider"><h3>Other Disorders to Consider</h3><p><b>Leigh syndrome</b> is a progressive neurodegenerative disorder characterized by hypotonia, spasticity, dystonia, muscle weakness, hypo- or hyperreflexia, seizures, movement disorders, cerebellar ataxia, and peripheral neuropathy. In individuals with Leigh syndrome, MRI changes most often occur initially in the brain stem, and the gliosis "migrates" over time to involve the deep gray masses and cortex, whereas in AHS the initial lesions form in the cerebral cortex (usually the occipital lobes), followed by the cerebellum, basal ganglia, thalamus, and brain stem. Epilepsia partialis continua (EPC), seen in Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome (AHS), has been described in individuals with Leigh syndrome [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.mameni_kien_.2017.74">Mameni&#x00161;kien&#x00117; &#x00026; Wolf 2017</a>]. Most individuals with Leigh syndrome have an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/x-linked/">X-linked</a> disorder of mitochondrial energy generation (see <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/leigh-nucl-ov/">Nuclear Gene-Encoded Leigh Syndrome Spectrum Overview</a>); Leigh syndrome can also be caused by genetic alternations in mitochondrial DNA (see <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/narp/">Mitochondrial DNA-Associated Leigh Syndrome and NARP</a> and <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/kss/">Single Large-Scale Mitochondrial DNA Deletion Syndromes</a>).</p><p>For additional disorders to consider in the differential diagnosis of individuals presenting with <b>ataxia</b>, see <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/ataxias/">Hereditary Ataxia Overview.</a></p><p>For additional disorders to consider in the differential diagnosis of individuals presenting with <b>peripheral neuropathy</b>, see <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/cmt/">Charcot-Marie-Tooth Hereditary Neuropathy Overview.</a></p></div></div><div id="alpers.Management"><h2 id="_alpers_Management_">Management</h2><p>No clinical practice guidelines for <i>POLG</i>-related disorders have been published, although consensus statement guidelines for primary mitochondrial diseases are available [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2017.10">Parikh et al 2017</a>].</p><div id="alpers.Evaluations_Following_Initial_Dia"><h3>Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</h3><p>To establish the extent of disease and needs in an individual diagnosed with <i>POLG</i>-related disorder, the evaluations summarized in <a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobalpersTpolgrelateddisordersrecommend">Table 5</a> (if not performed as part of the evaluation that led to diagnosis) are recommended. Evaluation should always include measures of functional neurologic status.</p><div id="alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 5. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>POLG</i>-Related Disorders: Recommended Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comment</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Neurologic eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To incl brain MRI&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider EEG &#x00026; video EEG if seizures are a concern.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Ataxia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Orthopedics&#x000a0;/ physical medicine &#x00026; rehab&#x000a0;/ PT &#x00026; OT eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To incl assessment of:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Gross motor &#x00026; fine motor skills</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Mobility, ADL, &#x00026; need for adaptive devices</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Need for PT (to improve gross motor skills) &#x00026;/or OT (to improve fine motor skills)</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Development</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Developmental assessment</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To incl motor, adaptive, cognitive, &#x00026; speech-language eval</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eval for early intervention&#x000a0;/ special education, need for speech therapy</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurobehavioral/</b>
<br />
<b>Psychiatric</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Neuropsychiatric eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For persons age &#x0003e;12 mos: screening for concerns incl sleep disturbances, ADHD, anxiety, &#x00026;/or findings suggestive of ASD. Formal neuropsychological eval can be considered for those w/any concerns identified on screening.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Depression screen</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for mood disturbances.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Musculoskeletal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Orthopedics&#x000a0;/ physical medicine &#x00026; rehab&#x000a0;/ PT &#x00026; OT eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To incl assessment of:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Gross motor &#x00026; fine motor skills</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Contractures, clubfoot, &#x00026; kyphoscoliosis</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Mobility, ADL, &#x00026; need for adaptive devices</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Need for PT (to improve gross motor skills) &#x00026;/or OT (to improve fine motor skills)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consideration of exercise program</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Gastrointestinal/</b>
<br />
<b>Feeding</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Gastroenterology&#x000a0;/ nutrition&#x000a0;/ feeding team eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To incl liver function tests incl ALT &#x00026; AST; serum concentrations of ammonia, glutamine &#x00026; tyrosine, bilirubin, albumin, &#x00026; cholesterol; fasting blood glucose levels; &#x00026; coagulation factors (prothrombin time or INR)&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consideration of liver ultrasound to evaluate for liver fibrosis</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To incl eval of aspiration risk incl swallow study for bulbar symptoms &#x00026; nutritional status</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider eval for gastrostomy tube placement in persons w/dysphagia &#x00026;/or aspiration risk.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Eyes</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ophthalmologic eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To assess for reduced vision, abnormal ocular movement, best corrected visual acuity, refractive errors, strabismus, &#x00026; more complex findings (e.g., cataract, retinal dystrophy) that may require referral for subspecialty care &#x00026;/or low vision services</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hearing</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Audiologic eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for hearing loss.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>ENT/Mouth</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">ENT eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">A sleep study is recommended to evaluate for central or obstructive apnea or hypopnea that results in either pCO<sub>2</sub> elevation or O<sub>2</sub> desaturation.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cardiovascular</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Cardiac eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To incl echocardiogram &#x00026; electrocardiogram</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Respiratory</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Pulmonary eval</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To incl baseline pulmonary function testing</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Screen for pancreatic, thyroid, &#x00026; adrenal function.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider if clinically indicated &#x00026; in critical illness.&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Pregnancy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">High-risk obstetrical care</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To incl discussion between managing physicians</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Genetic counseling</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By genetics professionals&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To obtain a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pedigree/">pedigree</a> &#x00026; inform affected persons &#x00026; their families re nature, MOI, &#x00026; implications of <i>POLG</i>-related disorders to facilitate medical &#x00026; personal decision making</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family support</b>
<br />
<b>&#x00026; resources</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By clinicians, wider care team, &#x00026; family support organizations</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment of family &#x00026; social structure to determine need for:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Community or <a href="#alpers.Prenatal_Testing_and_Preimplantat">online resources</a> such as <a href="https://www.p2pusa.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Parent to Parent</a></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Social work involvement for parental support</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Home nursing referral</div></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ADHD = attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder; ADL = activities of daily living; ALT = alanine transaminase; ASD = autism spectrum disorder; AST = aspartate aminotransferase; INR = international normalized ratio; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a>; OT = occupational therapy; pCO<sub>2</sub>: partial pressure of carbon dioxide; PT = physical therapy</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.5.1"><p class="no_margin">In some instances, the first neuroimaging study may be normal, but with certain phenotypes, such as Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome, changes may be seen in a relatively short amount of time.</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.5.2"><p class="no_margin">AST elevation, and to a lesser extent ALT elevation, may be due to muscle disease. Therefore, simultaneously obtaining a serum CK level helps differentiate between liver &#x00026; muscle involvement, which can both be seen in individuals with <i>POLG</i>-related disorders.</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.5.3"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2017.10">Parikh et al [2017]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.5.4"><p class="no_margin">Clinical geneticist, certified genetic counselor, certified genetic nurse, genetics advanced practice provider (nurse practitioner or physician assistant)</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="alpers.Treatment_of_Manifestations"><h3>Treatment of Manifestations</h3><p>There is no cure for <i>POLG</i>-related disorders. Supportive care to improve quality of life, maximize function, and reduce complications is recommended. This ideally involves multidisciplinary care by specialists in relevant fields (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobalpersTpolgrelateddisorderstreatment">Table 6</a>).</p><div id="alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 6. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>POLG</i>-related disorders: Treatment of Manifestations</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestation/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Considerations/Other</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Developmental delay&#x000a0;/</b>
<br />
<b>Intellectual disability&#x000a0;/</b>
<br />
<b>Neurobehavioral issues</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See <a href="#alpers.Developmental_Delay__Intellectual">Developmental Delay&#x000a0;/ Intellectual Disability Management Issues</a>.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">OT, PT, &#x00026; ST are indicated in AHS to maintain or improve neurologic function for as long as possible &#x00026; for comfort care.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Epilepsy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standardized treatment w/ASM by experienced neurologist</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Many ASMs may be effective; none has been demonstrated effective specifically for this disorder.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Note: Valproic acid (Depakene<sup>&#x000ae;</sup>) &#x00026; sodium divalproate (divalproex) (Depakote<sup>&#x000ae;</sup>) should be avoided (see <a href="#alpers.AgentsCircumstances_to_Avoid">Agents/Circumstances to Avoid</a>). Because other ASMs may also accelerate liver deterioration, it is reasonable to monitor liver enzymes every 2-4 weeks after introducing any new ASM.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment of epilepsy is similar to non-mitochondrial disease w/seizures. Lamotrigine may worsen myoclonic seizures.&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup> Vigabatrin may need to be avoided in persons w/mtDNA depletion because it inhibits conversion of deoxyribonucleoside diphosphate to deoxyribonucleoside triphosphate in the mitochondrial nucleoside salvage pathway &#x00026; may worsen mtDNA depletion.&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup> Topiramate may worsen acidosis.&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Refractory epilepsy, esp EPC, may be impossible to control w/any treatment. In persons w/EPC, use of high-dose ASM may control the clinical seizures, but assoc obtundation &#x00026; subsequent risk of aspiration &#x00026; ventilatory failure may outweigh the benefit.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Education of parents/caregivers&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Movement disorders</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment by an experienced neurologist</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Myoclonus &#x00026; other non-epileptic movement disorders occur as part of AHS. The use of benzodiazepines often &#x02193; severity of abnormal movements &#x00026; also assists in seizure control &#x00026; reduction of spasticity.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Chorea &#x00026; athetosis may cause pain, &#x00026; treatment w/muscle relaxants &#x00026; pain medications, incl narcotics, is advised. Some movement disorders can be treated w/dopaminergic medication such as levodopa-carbidopa or tetrabenazine; a trial of either of these medications can be considered.&#x000a0;<sup>5</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Spasticity</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Orthopedics&#x000a0;/ physical medicine &#x00026; rehab&#x000a0;/ PT &#x00026; OT incl stretching to help avoid contractures &#x00026; falls</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider need for positioning &#x00026; mobility devices, disability parking placard.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Botulinum toxin can be used w/caution (w/consideration of systemic effects).&#x000a0;<sup>6</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Inadequate weight gain &#x00026; linear growth (children) or unexplained weight loss (all ages)</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Feeding therapy</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Gastrostomy tube placement may be required for persistent feeding issues.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Low threshold for renal eval in persons w/inadequate weight gain &#x00026; linear growth or unexplained weight loss</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A feeding eval &#x00026;/or radiographic swallowing study should be considered when showing clinical signs or symptoms of dysphagia.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>GI &#x00026; bowel dysfunction</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment by GI specialist for feeding or nutritional issues</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Placement of a gastric feeding tube when appropriate can maintain nutritional status &#x00026;/or prevent aspiration.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor for constipation.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Stool softeners, prokinetics, osmotic agents, or laxatives as needed</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Liver failure</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment may incl small, frequent meals or continuous feeding to compensate for defective gluconeogenesis; reduction in dietary protein; use of non-absorbable sugars to create an osmotic diarrhea; &#x00026; use of conjugating agents to treat hyperammonemia.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Levocarnitine may have some benefit in the setting of liver failure &#x00026; has a low risk, yet there is no evidence for routine use.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Eyes</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standardized treatment by ophthalmologist</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For refractive errors, strabismus, &#x00026; more complex findings (e.g., cataract, retinal dystrophy)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ptosis</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Surgery for ptosis may provide symptomatic relief for some persons.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Low vision services</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Children: through early intervention programs &#x00026;/or school district</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Adults: low vision clinic &#x00026;/or community vision services&#x000a0;/ OT&#x000a0;/ mobility services</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cerebral visual impairment</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">No specific treatment</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consideration of referral to a low vision clinic</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hearing</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Hearing aids may be helpful per otolaryngologist</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Community hearing services through early intervention or school district</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Respiratory</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatment by pulmonologist for respiratory issues</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Tracheostomy placement &#x00026; artificial ventilation may be performed as needed.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assessment of nocturnal ventilatory function can be performed for evidence of central &#x00026;/or obstructive apnea using polysomnography w/measurement of pCO<sub>2</sub> &#x00026; monitoring by pulse oximetry. Treatment w/CPAP, BiPAP, or more invasive ventilatory therapy as indicated.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family/Community</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ensure appropriate social work involvement to connect families w/local resources, respite, &#x00026; support.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Coordinate care to manage multiple subspecialty appointments, equipment, medications, &#x00026; supplies.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_treatment_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ongoing assessment of need for palliative care involvement &#x00026;/or home nursing (esp for persons w/AHS)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider involvement in adaptive sports or <a href="https://www.specialolympics.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Special Olympics</a>.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">AHS = Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome; ASM = anti-seizure medication; BiPAP = bilevel positive airway pressure; CPAP = continuous positive airway pressure; EPC = epilepsia partialis continua; OT = occupational therapy; PT = physical therapy; ST = speech therapy</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.6.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.hikmat.a">Hikmat et al [2017a]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.6.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.besse.2015.417">Besse et al [2015]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.6.3"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.mirza.2011.297">Mirza et al [2011]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.6.4"><p class="no_margin">Education of parents/caregivers regarding common seizure presentations is appropriate. For information on non-medical interventions and coping strategies for children diagnosed with epilepsy, see <a href="https://www.epilepsy.com/tools-resources/forms-resources#Epilepsy-Foundation-Toolbox" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Epilepsy Foundation Toolbox</a>.</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.6.5"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.hakonen.2005.430">Hakonen et al [2005]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>6. </dt><dd><div id="alpers.TF.6.6"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2017.10">Parikh et al [2017]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><div id="alpers.Developmental_Delay__Intellectual"><h4>Developmental Delay / Intellectual Disability Management Issues</h4><p>The following information represents typical management recommendations for individuals with developmental delay&#x000a0;/ intellectual disability in the United States; standard recommendations may vary from country to country.</p><p><b>Ages 0-3 years.</b> Referral to an early intervention program is recommended for access to occupational, physical, speech, and feeding therapy as well as infant mental health services, special educators, and sensory impairment specialists. In the US, early intervention is a federally funded program available in all states that provides in-home services to target individual therapy needs.</p><p><b>Ages 3-5 years.</b> In the US, developmental preschool through the local public school district is recommended. Before placement, an evaluation is made to determine needed services and therapies and an individualized education plan (IEP) is developed for those who qualify based on established motor, language, social, or cognitive delay. The early intervention program typically assists with this transition. Developmental preschool is center based; for children too medically unstable to attend, home-based services are provided.</p><p><b>All ages.</b> Consultation with a developmental pediatrician is recommended to ensure the involvement of appropriate community, state, and educational agencies (US) and to support parents in maximizing quality of life. Some issues to consider:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>IEP services:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>An IEP provides specially designed instruction and related services to children who qualify.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>IEP services will be reviewed annually to determine whether any changes are needed.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Special education law requires that children participating in an IEP be in the least restrictive environment feasible at school and included in general education as much as possible, when and where appropriate.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Vision and hearing consultants should be a part of the child's IEP team to support access to academic material.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>PT, OT, and speech services will be provided in the IEP to the extent that the need affects the child's access to academic material. Beyond that, private supportive therapies based on the affected individual's needs may be considered. Specific recommendations regarding type of therapy can be made by a developmental pediatrician.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>As a child enters the teen years, a transition plan should be discussed and incorporated in the IEP. For those receiving IEP services, the public school district is required to provide services until age 21.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A 504 plan (Section 504: a US federal statute that prohibits discrimination based on disability) can be considered for those who require accommodations or modifications such as front-of-class seating, assistive technology devices, classroom scribes, extra time between classes, modified assignments, and enlarged text.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Developmental Disabilities Administration (DDA) enrollment is recommended. DDA is a US public agency that provides services and support to qualified individuals. Eligibility differs by state but is typically determined by diagnosis and/or associated cognitive/adaptive disabilities.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Families with limited income and resources may also qualify for supplemental security income (SSI) for their child with a disability.</div></li></ul></div><div id="alpers.Motor_Dysfunction"><h4>Motor Dysfunction</h4><p>
<b>Gross motor dysfunction</b>
