</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Physical therapy is recommended to maximize mobility and to reduce the risk for later-onset orthopedic complications (e.g., contractures, scoliosis, hip dislocation).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider use of durable medical equipment and positioning devices as needed (e.g., wheelchairs, walkers, bath chairs, orthotics, adaptive strollers).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>For muscle tone abnormalities including hypertonia or dystonia, consider involving appropriate specialists to aid in management of baclofen, tizanidine, Botox<sup>&#x000ae;</sup>, anti-parkinsonian medications, or orthopedic procedures.</div></li></ul><p><b>Fine motor dysfunction.</b> Occupational therapy is recommended for difficulty with fine motor skills that affect adaptive function such as feeding, grooming, dressing, and writing.</p><p><b>Oral motor dysfunction</b> should be assessed at each visit and clinical feeding evaluations and/or radiographic swallowing studies should be obtained for choking/gagging during feeds, poor weight gain, frequent respiratory illnesses, or feeding refusal that is not otherwise explained. Assuming that the child is safe to eat by mouth, feeding therapy (typically from an occupational or speech therapist) is recommended to help improve coordination or sensory-related feeding issues. Feeds can be thickened or chilled for safety. When feeding dysfunction is severe, an NG-tube or G-tube may be necessary.</p><p><b>Communication issues.</b> Consider evaluation for alternative means of communication (e.g., <a href="https://www.asha.org/NJC/AAC/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">augmentative and alternative communication</a> [AAC]) for individuals who have expressive language difficulties. An AAC evaluation can be completed by a speech-language pathologist who has expertise in the area. The evaluation will consider cognitive abilities and sensory impairments to determine the most appropriate form of communication. AAC devices can range from low-tech, such as picture exchange communication, to high-tech, such as voice-generating devices. Contrary to popular belief, AAC devices do not hinder verbal development of speech, but rather support optimal speech and language development.</p></div><div id="alpers.NeurobehavioralPsychiatric_Concer"><h4>Neurobehavioral/Psychiatric Concerns</h4><p>Children may qualify for and benefit from interventions used in treatment of autism spectrum disorder, including applied behavior analysis (ABA). ABA therapy is targeted to the individual child's behavioral, social, and adaptive strengths and weaknesses and typically performed one on one with a board-certified behavior analyst.</p><p>Consultation with a developmental pediatrician may be helpful in guiding parents through appropriate behavior management strategies or providing prescription medications, such as medication used to treat attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder, when necessary.</p><p>Concerns about serious aggressive or destructive behavior can be addressed by a pediatric psychiatrist.</p></div></div><div id="alpers.Surveillance"><h3>Surveillance</h3><p>To monitor existing manifestations, the individual's response to supportive care, and the emergence of new manifestations, evaluations by a multidisciplinary team including neurologist, biochemical geneticist, hepatologist or gastroenterologist, physiatrist, psychiatrist, neuropsychologist and/or psychologist, ophthalmologist, and pulmonologist are recommended. No standard-of-care guidelines regarding the recommended frequency of evaluations exist; surveillance should be guided by clinical features, and the schedule should be modified if the clinical course is stable. For those with the most severe phenotypes, the recommendations in <a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobalpersTpolgrelateddisordersrecommend1">Table 7</a> can be considered.</p><div id="alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 7. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>POLG</i>-related disorders: Recommended Surveillance</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Frequency</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Monitor those w/seizures as clinically indicated.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess for new manifestations such as seizures, changes in tone, &#x00026; movement disorders.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>EEG &#x00026; video EEG monitoring (e.g., for suspicion of subclinical status epilepticus or EPC, to determine if events are seizures or non-epileptic movements)</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="5" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Development</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor developmental progress &#x00026; educational needs.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurobehavioral/</b>
<br />
<b>Psychiatric</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment for anxiety, ADHD, ASD, aggression, &#x00026; depression</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Musculoskeletal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Physical medicine, OT/PT assessment of mobility, self-help skills</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Feeding</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Measurement of growth parameters</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eval of nutritional status &#x00026; safety of oral intake</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Liver function &#x00026; metabolic status</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Complete blood count</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Electrolytes</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Liver enzymes (AST, ALT, GGT)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>CK level</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Liver function tests incl preprandial serum glucose level; serum concentration of ammonia, albumin, bilirubin (free &#x00026; conjugated), &#x00026; cholesterol; coagulation factors incl prothrombin time or INR</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Every 3 mos, or as clinically indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Urine analysis</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Lactic acid levels</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Liver ultrasound</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="2" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually or as clinically indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Plasma amino acids</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Plasma concentration of free &#x00026; total carnitine (unless treated w/levocarnitine, in which case measure annually)</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Gastrointestinal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor for swallowing dysfunction through barium swallow study, gastrointestinal paresis, constipation, feeding intolerance, or malabsorption.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit or as clinically indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Respiratory</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor for evidence of aspiration &#x00026; respiratory insufficiency.</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Polysomnogram w/CPAP titration as part of an eval of subacute mental status changes, apnea, or snoring</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">As clinically indicated or every 2-3 yrs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Auditory</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Audiogram or brain stem auditory evoked responses</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">As clinically indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Ophthalmologic involvement</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Routine ophthalmologic exam</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Per treating ophthalmologist(s)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Low vision services</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Per treating clinicians</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cardiovascular</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Routine cardiac exam</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Laboratory testing for thyroid or adrenal dysfunction</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Upon critical illness or as clinically indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family/Community</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">Assess family need for social work support (e.g., palliative/respite care, home nursing, other local resources), care coordination, or follow-up <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> if new questions arise (e.g., family planning).</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polgrelated_disorders_recommend_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ADHD = attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder; ALT = alanine transaminase; ASD = autism spectrum disorder; AST = aspartate transaminase; CK = creatine kinase; EPC = epilepsia partialis continua; GGT = gamma-glutamyl transferase; INR = international normalized ratio; OT = occupational therapy; PT = physical therapy</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="alpers.AgentsCircumstances_to_Avoid"><h3>Agents/Circumstances to Avoid</h3><p>Valproic acid (Depakene<sup>&#x000ae;</sup>) and sodium divalproate (divalproex) (Depakote<sup>&#x000ae;</sup>) should be avoided because of the risk of precipitating and/or accelerating liver disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.saneto.2010.140">Saneto et al 2010</a>].</p><p>As with some other mitochondrial diseases, physical stressors such as infection, fever, dehydration, and anorexia can result in a sudden deterioration and should be avoided if possible.</p></div><div id="alpers.Evaluation_of_Relatives_at_Risk"><h3>Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</h3><p>See <a href="#alpers.Related_Genetic_Counseling_Issues">Genetic Counseling</a> for issues related to testing of at-risk relatives for <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> purposes.</p></div><div id="alpers.Therapies_Under_Investigation"><h3>Therapies Under Investigation</h3><p>Search <a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ClinicalTrials.gov</a> in the US and <a href="https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/search" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">EU Clinical Trials Register</a> in Europe for information on clinical studies for a wide range of diseases and conditions. Note: There may not be clinical trials for this disorder.</p></div><div id="alpers.Other"><h3>Other</h3><p>Liver transplantation is not advised in children with Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome (AHS) because transplanting the liver does not alter the rapid progression of the brain disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.kelly.2000.170">Kelly 2000</a>].</p><p>However, liver transplantation in adults who have an acceptable quality of life may be of benefit.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In one report, one of two individuals undergoing liver transplantation survived [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.tzoulis.2006.1685">Tzoulis et al 2006</a>]. In another report of solid organ transplantation in primary mitochondrial disease, of six individuals with <i>POLG</i>-related disease, two survived without complications [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2016.178">Parikh et al 2016</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In another report, a woman underwent liver transplantation at age 19 years, eight years after experiencing fulminant hepatic failure following onset of valproate therapy. Molecular genetic testing seven years after her liver transplantation confirmed the diagnosis of a <i>POLG</i>-related disorder; her <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> fit best with SANDO [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.wong.2008.e150">Wong et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2016.178">Parikh et al 2016</a>].</div></li></ul><p>The use of other treatments for refractory epilepsy, such as corticotropin or prednisone, ketogenic diet, and intravenous immunoglobulin G, are unproven in the treatment of AHS. The following, however, may be considered:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Vitamin and cofactor therapy with the intent to fortify mitochondrial function may be offered, yet there is insufficient evidence demonstrating objective benefit in cohorts of persons. There have not been formal studies of the use of these vitamins and cofactors in AHS or other <i>POLG</i>-related disorders [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2009.414">Parikh et al 2009</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2015.689">Parikh et al 2015</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.camp.2016.187">Camp et al 2016</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The use of folinic acid should be considered [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.hasselmann.2010.58">Hasselmann et al 2010</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The use of levoarginine has been reported to be helpful in reducing the frequency and severity of the strokes associated with <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/melas/">MELAS</a>, and can be considered for use in persons with <i>POLG</i>-related disorders, especially if deficiency in the plasma or cerebrospinal fluid arginine concentration is confirmed [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.elhattab.2017.1">El-Hattab et al 2017</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The use of levocarnitine should be reserved for individuals with reduced free carnitine levels in the blood, and the levels should be monitored [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2015.689">Parikh et al 2015</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Creatine monohydrate, coenzyme Q<sub>10</sub>, B vitamins, and antioxidants such as alpha-lipoic acid, vitamin E, and vitamin C have been used as mitochondrial supplements based on limited case reports and small series but with a lack of objective evidence based on randomized controlled trials. Use of all in <i>POLG</i>-related disorders is reasonable given the general lack of toxicity but is not mandatory [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.gold.2001.309">Gold &#x00026; Cohen 2001</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.rodriguez.2007.235">Rodriguez et al 2007</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.horvath.2008.558">Horvath et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2013.681">Parikh et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.parikh.2015.689">Parikh et al 2015</a>].</div></li></ul></div></div><div id="alpers.Genetic_Counseling"><h2 id="_alpers_Genetic_Counseling_">Genetic Counseling</h2><p>
<i>Genetic counseling is the process of providing individuals and families with
information on the nature, mode(s) of inheritance, and implications of genetic disorders to help them
make informed medical and personal decisions. The following section deals with genetic
risk assessment and the use of family history and genetic testing to clarify genetic
status for family members; it is not meant to address all personal, cultural, or
ethical issues that may arise or to substitute for consultation with a genetics
professional</i>. &#x02014;ED.</p><div id="alpers.Mode_of_Inheritance"><h3>Mode of Inheritance</h3><p>Early-onset and juvenile/adult-onset <i>POLG</i>-related disorders are typically caused by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants and inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> manner. Late-onset progressive external ophthalmoplegia (PEO) may be caused by a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a> manner.</p><p>Note: Digenic inheritance involving pathogenic variants in <i>POLG</i> and <i>TWNK</i> has been reported in two individuals with PEO [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.van_goethem.2003a.175">Van Goethem et al 2003a</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.da_pozzo.2015.1713">Da Pozzo et al 2015</a>].</p></div><div id="alpers.Autosomal_Recessive_Inheritance"><h3>Autosomal Recessive Inheritance &#x02013; Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The parents of an affected child are presumed to be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Molecular genetic testing is recommended for the parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> to confirm that both parents are <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and to allow reliable <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> assessment.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is detected in only one parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, it is possible that one of the pathogenic variants identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> occurred as a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> event in the proband [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.chan.2009.340">Chan et al 2009</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.lutz.2009.126">Lutz et al 2009</a>] or as a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/postzygotic/">postzygotic</a> <i>de novo</i> event in a mosaic parent. If the proband appears to have <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> pathogenic variants (i.e., the same two pathogenic variants), additional possibilities to consider include:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A single- or multiexon <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> that was not detected by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a> and resulted in the artifactual appearance of homozygosity;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Uniparental isodisomy for the parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/chromosome/">chromosome</a> with the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that resulted in homozygosity for the pathogenic variant in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Heterozygous parents of a child with an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> <i>POLG</i>-related disorder are typically asymptomatic.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If both parents are known to be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of inheriting <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants and being affected, a 50% chance of being heterozygous, and a 25% chance of inheriting neither of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>POLG</i> pathogenic variants.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The <i>POLG</i>-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> is usually similar in affected family members; less commonly, affected sibs may present differently in terms of age of onset, specific clinical features, and severity. For example, in a family in which affected sibs were compound heterozygotes (<i>POLG</i> pathogenic variants <a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobalpersTpolgpathogenicvariantsrefere">p.Gly848Ser</a> and <a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobalpersTpolgpathogenicvariantsrefere">p.Trp748Ser</a>), one sib presented with developmental delays and status epilepticus at age three years, while the other sib presented with ataxia and myoclonus in early adolescence [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.tang.2011.669">Tang et al 2011</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Heterozygous sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> with an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> <i>POLG</i>-related disorder are typically asymptomatic.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Offspring of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Unless an affected individual's reproductive partner also has <i>POLG</i>-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>(s), offspring will be obligate heterozygotes (carriers) for a pathogenic variant in <i>POLG</i> (see <b>Family planning</b>).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Individuals with early-onset <i>POLG</i>-related disorders (e.g., Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome and childhood myocerebrohepatopathy spectrum) are not known to reproduce.</div></li></ul><p><b>Other family members.</b> Each sib of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s parents is at a 50% risk of being <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</p><p><b>Carrier (<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygote/">heterozygote</a>) detection.</b> Carrier testing for at-risk relatives requires prior identification of the <i>POLG</i> pathogenic variants in the family.</p></div><div id="alpers.Autosomal_Dominant_Inheritance__R"><h3>Autosomal Dominant Inheritance &#x02013; Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Most individuals with PEO caused by a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> (i.e., <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a> PEO or adPEO) have an affected parent, although age of onset and severity of presentation can vary greatly from generation to generation.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Some individuals diagnosed with adPEO have the disorder as the result of a <i>de novo POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>. The proportion of probands who have a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> pathogenic variant is unknown but thought to be low (&#x0003c;1%).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> appears to be the only affected family member (i.e., a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/simplex/">simplex</a> case), recommendations for the evaluation of the parents include <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> for the <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the proband, a complete family history, and physical examination focusing on the most common features of <i>POLG</i>-related disease (ophthalmoplegia, myopathy, ataxia, and neuropathy). Note: Because migraine, depression, gastrointestinal problems, fatigue, exercise intolerance, and seizures are common in the general population, their presence as <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/isolated/">isolated</a> findings is not likely to be relevant.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is not identified in either parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, the following possibilities should be considered:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> has a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> inherited a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> from a parent with <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a> (or somatic and germline) <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mosaicism/">mosaicism</a>. Note: Testing of parental leukocyte DNA may not detect all instances of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/somatic-mosaicism/">somatic mosaicism</a> and will not detect a pathogenic variant that is present in the germ cells only.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Evaluation of parents may determine that one is affected but has escaped previous diagnosis because of a milder phenotypic presentation, failure to recognize the disorder in family members, early death of the parent before the onset of symptoms, or late onset of the disease in the affected parent. Therefore, an apparently negative family history cannot be confirmed unless <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> has demonstrated that neither parent is <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</div></li></ul><p><b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</b> The risk to the sibs of the proband depends on the genetic status of the proband's parents:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a parent of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is affected and/or is known to have the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the proband, the risk to the sibs of inheriting the pathogenic variant is 50%. The <i>POLG</i>-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> is generally similar in affected family members, although the age of onset can vary significantly. Data on <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/penetrance/">penetrance</a> of adPEO are not available.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is not detected in the leukocyte DNA of either parent, the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> to sibs is estimated to be 1% because of the possibility of parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline-mosaicism/">germline mosaicism</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.rahbari.2016.126">Rahbari et al 2016</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the parents have not been tested for the <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> but are clinically unaffected, sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> are still presumed to be at increased risk for adPEO because of the possibility of late onset in a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> parent or the theoretic possibility of parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline-mosaicism/">germline mosaicism</a>.</div></li></ul><p><b>Offspring of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</b> Each child of an individual with <i>POLG</i>-related adPEO has a 50% chance of inheriting the <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</p><p><b>Other family members.</b> The risk to other family members depends on the status of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s parents: if a parent has a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, the parent's family members may be at risk.</p></div><div id="alpers.Related_Genetic_Counseling_Issues"><h3>Related Genetic Counseling Issues</h3><p><b>At-risk family members.</b> Sibs who are close in age to or younger than the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> may still be at risk and in need of diagnostic evaluation. Note: Because the age of onset, even among family members with identical <i>POLG</i> pathogenic variants, can vary considerably, there is no firm certainty as to how many years need to pass before there is no longer a risk of <i>POLG</i>-related features in a sib [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.rahman.2019.40">Rahman &#x00026; Copeland 2019</a>].</p><p>
<b>Family planning</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The optimal time for determination of genetic risk and discussion of the availability of prenatal/<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> is before pregnancy.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>It is appropriate to offer <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> (including discussion of potential risks to offspring and reproductive options) to young adults who are affected or are at risk of having a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Carrier testing should be considered for the reproductive partners of individuals known to have a <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, particularly if <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/consanguinity/">consanguinity</a> is likely and/or if both partners are of the same ancestry.</div></li></ul></div><div id="alpers.Prenatal_Testing_and_Preimplantat"><h3>Prenatal Testing and Preimplantation Genetic Testing</h3><p>Once the <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>(s) have been identified in an affected family member, prenatal and <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> for <i>POLG</i>-related disorders are possible.</p><p>Differences in perspective may exist among medical professionals and within families regarding the use of prenatal and <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a>. While most health care professionals would consider use of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/prenatal-testing/">prenatal testing</a> to be a personal decision, discussion of these issues may be helpful.</p></div></div><div id="alpers.Resources"><h2 id="_alpers_Resources_">Resources</h2><p>

<a href="/omim/157640" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=entrez&amp;targetcat=term&amp;targettype=omim">157640</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">PROGRESSIVE EXTERNAL OPHTHALMOPLEGIA WITH MITOCHONDRIAL DNA DELETIONS, AUTOSOMAL DOMINANT 1; PEOA1</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">
<a href="/omim/174763" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=entrez&amp;targetcat=term&amp;targettype=omim">174763</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">POLYMERASE, DNA, GAMMA; POLG</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">
<a href="/omim/203700" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=entrez&amp;targetcat=term&amp;targettype=omim">203700</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">MITOCHONDRIAL DNA DEPLETION SYNDROME 4A (ALPERS TYPE); MTDPS4A</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">
<a href="/omim/258450" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=entrez&amp;targetcat=term&amp;targettype=omim">258450</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">PROGRESSIVE EXTERNAL OPHTHALMOPLEGIA WITH MITOCHONDRIAL DNA DELETIONS, AUTOSOMAL RECESSIVE 1; PEOB1</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">
<a href="/omim/607459" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=entrez&amp;targetcat=term&amp;targettype=omim">607459</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">SENSORY ATAXIC NEUROPATHY, DYSARTHRIA, AND OPHTHALMOPARESIS; SANDO</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">
<a href="/omim/613662" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=entrez&amp;targetcat=term&amp;targettype=omim">613662</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">MITOCHONDRIAL DNA DEPLETION SYNDROME 4B (MNGIE TYPE); MTDPS4B</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><div id="alpers.Molecular_Pathogenesis"><h3>Molecular Pathogenesis</h3><p>The mitochondrion comprises almost 1,500 proteins, but only the 13 that comprise small portions of the respiratory chain complexes I, III, IV, and V are encoded by the mitochondrial genome. The mitochondrial genome is a circular molecule that in humans contains 15,569 base pairs including 37 genes &#x02013; 13 genes encoding for subunits of complexes I, III, IV, and V, as well as 22 tRNAs and 2 rRNAs &#x02013; that are distinct and necessary for mitochondrial translation. The terminal portion of energy production occurs in the respiratory chain; disruption of the production and/or assembly of any component leads to a deficiency of ATP and resultant cellular energy failure. The cause of clinical symptoms likely includes insufficient ATP production, but also excessive free radical production, disturbed calcium handling, and other factors. Unlike nuclear DNA, mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA) replicates continuously and independently of cell division. Polymerase (pol) gamma is the major DNA polymerase in humans required for replication and repair of mtDNA. Replication of mtDNA requires a heterotrimer of one catalytic subunit of pol gamma and two accessory subunits, encoded by <i>POLG2</i>, that assist in binding and processing the synthesized DNA. The twinkle protein, encoded by <i>TWNK</i>, functions as the 5' &#x02192; 3' DNA helicase.</p><p><i>POLG</i> encodes DNA pol gamma, which has three functional domains:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Exonuclease, responsible for proofreading (first third of the protein)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Linker region (center of the protein)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Polymerase, responsible for replication (last third of the protein)</div></li></ul><p>The clinical features <i>POLG</i>-related disorders most likely result from mtDNA depletion or multiple mtDNA deletions [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.lujan.2020.248">Lujan et al 2020</a>] over time of normal mtDNA, with resultant reduced electron transport chain activity. The adPEO-causing pathogenic variants cluster in the active site region of the DNA polymerase.</p><p><b>Mechanism of disease causation.</b> Loss of <i>POLG</i> function results in loss of polymerase activity &#x02013; resulting in loss of mtDNA &#x02013; or loss of endonuclease function &#x02013; resulting in non-fidelity of mtDNA replication &#x02013; or both. The loss of mtDNA results in loss of normal mitochondrial translation as well as loss of function involving mtDNA-encoded subunits found in complexes I, III, IV, and V. The result is loss of normal ATP production and elevated free radical production and other mitochondrial functions, with resultant injury to neurons and other cells.</p><div id="alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 8. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p><i>POLG</i> Pathogenic Variants Referenced in This <i>GeneReview</i></p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK26471/table/alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Reference Sequences</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">DNA Nucleotide Change</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Predicted Protein Change</th><th id="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_4" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comment [Reference]</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_1" rowspan="5" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/nucleotide/NM_002693.3" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NM_002693<wbr style="display:inline-block"></wbr>​.3</a>
<br />
<a href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/protein/NP_002684.1" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NP_002684<wbr style="display:inline-block"></wbr>​.1</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">c.695G&#x0003e;A</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">p.Arg232His</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Pathogenic variant that causes AHS when in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a> state w/the p.[Trp748Ser;p.Glu1143Gly] haplotype</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">c.1399G&#x0003e;A</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">p.Ala467Thr</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Common <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in <i>POLG</i>-related disorders; severely &#x02193; DNA polymerase (pol) gamma activity (4% of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/wild_type/">wild type</a> pol gamma activity) by &#x02193; affinity for dNTPs &#x00026; lowering catalytic activity [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.chan.2005.31341">Chan et al 2005</a>].</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">c.1760C&#x0003e;T</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">p.Pro587Leu</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Common <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>; nearly always occurs w/p.Thr251Ile on the same <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/allele/">allele</a> (i.e., p.[Thr251Ile;Pro587Leu]) in affected persons &#x00026; in general population. Individually, these variants cause an approximately 30% reduction in DNA polymerase activity, but together there is a synergistic impairment of polymerase function to levels about 5% of normal [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.debalsi.2017.4198">DeBalsi et al 2017</a>].</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">c.2243G&#x0003e;C</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">p.Trp748Ser</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Common <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that causes AHS, ANS, arPEO, &#x00026; ataxia-neuropathy. Results in &#x02193; DNA polymerase activity, low processivity, &#x00026; severe DNA binding defect, but normal POLG2 interactions [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.chan.2006.3473">Chan et al 2006</a>]. Note: (1) The p.Glu1143Gly variant on the same <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/allele/">allele</a> modulates the deleterious effect of p.Trp748Ser by partially rescuing activity &#x00026; &#x02193; protein stability [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.chan.2006.3473">Chan et al 2006</a>]. AHS results when the p.[Trp748Ser;p.Glu1143Gly] haplotype occurs with a different <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> on the other <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/allele/">allele</a> (e.g., p.Arg232His). (2) This <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is a <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/founder_finnish/">Finnish founder variant</a>.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">c.2542G&#x0003e;A</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">p.Gly848Ser</td><td headers="hd_h_alpers.T.polg_pathogenic_variants_refere_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Common <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that results in &#x0003c;1% polymerase activity &#x00026; a defect in DNA binding function [<a class="bk_pop" href="#alpers.REF.kasiviswanathan.2009.19501">Kasiviswanathan et al 2009</a>]</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">Variants listed in the table have been provided by the authors. <i>GeneReviews</i> staff have not independently verified the classification of variants.</p></div></dd><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin"><i>GeneReviews</i> follows the standard naming conventions of the Human Genome Variation Society (<a href="https://varnomen.hgvs.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">varnomen<wbr style="display:inline-block"></wbr>​.hgvs.org</a>). See <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/app3/">Quick Reference</a> for an explanation of nomenclature.</p></div></dd><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">AHS = Alpers-Huttenlocher syndrome; ANS = ataxia neuropathy spectrum; arPEO = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> progressive external ophthalmoplegia; dNTP = deoxyribonucleotide triphosphate</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><p>An up-to-date listing of all pathogenic variants is available at <a href="https://tools.niehs.nih.gov/polg/index.cfm" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">tools.niehs.nih.gov</a>, managed by William Copeland, PhD.</p></div></div><div id="alpers.Chapter_Notes"><h2 id="_alpers_Chapter_Notes_">Chapter Notes</h2><div id="alpers.Author_Notes"><h3>Author Notes</h3><p><b>Bruce H Cohen</b> is a clinician caring for children and adults with mitochondrial disease since his training starting four decades ago. He first began caring for patients with <i>POLG</i> disease ten years before the <i>POLG</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> was cloned and characterized by Dr Copeland and has followed the work of the coauthors over the last 25 years. He has lectured hundreds of times to medical audiences and families on the topic of mitochondrial medicine. For the last decade his focus has been on clinical trials for mitochondrial disease.</p><p>Web pages: <a href="https://scholar.google.com/citations?user=9W9SRXEAAAAJ&#x00026;hl=en&#x00026;oi=ao" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">&#x0202a;&#x0202a;Google Scholar&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;</a>&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c;&#x0202c; and <a href="https://www.akronchildrens.org/people/Bruce-H-Cohen-MD.html#tab_!" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Akron Children's</a></p><p><b>William C Copeland</b> is a biochemist studying mitochondrial DNA replication. He has been leading the Mitochondrial DNA Replication group at the National Institute of Environmental Health Sciences for more than 30 years and is currently the Chief of the Genome Integrity and Structural Biology Laboratory. He uses biochemistry, enzyme kinetics, structural biology, and genetics to study the consequences of <i>POLG</i> pathogenic variants.</p><p>William C Copeland, PhD<br />Chief, Genome Integrity and Structural Biology Laboratory;<br />Principal Investigator, Mitochondrial DNA Replication Group,<br />National Institute of Environmental Health Sciences<br />111 TW Alexander Dr<br />Research Triangle Park, NC 27709<br />Phone: 984-287-4269<br />Email: <a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="vog.hin.shein@1nalepoc" class="oemail">vog.hin.shein@1nalepoc</a><br />Web page:<a href="http://www.niehs.nih.gov" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">www.niehs.nih.gov</a><br /><i>POLG</i> mutation database: <a href="https://tools.niehs.nih.gov/polg/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">tools.niehs.nih.gov</a></p><p><b>Patrick F Chinnery</b> is a neurologist and clinician-scientist caring for adults with mitochondrial disorders. He runs a laboratory and clinical research group studying disease mechanisms and developing new treatments for mitochondrial disorders based at the MRC Mitochondrial Biology Unit and Department of Clinical Neurosciences, University of Cambridge, United Kingdome.</p><p>Web pages: <a href="https://orcid.org/0000-0002-7065-6617" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ORCID</a>, <a href="https://www-neurosciences.medschl.cam.ac.uk/mitocamb/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Cambridge Clinical Mitochondrial Research Group</a>, and <a href="https://www.mrc-mbu.cam.ac.uk/research-groups/chinnery-group" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">MRC Mitochondrial Biology Unit &#x02013; Chinnery Group</a></p><p>Patrick F Chinnery, FRCP, FMedSci<br />Professor of Neurology, University of Cambridge<br />Department of Clinical Neurosciences<br />University Neurology Unit<br />Level 5 'A' Block, Box 165<br />Cambridge Biomedical Campus<br />Cambridge, CB2 0QQ, United Kingdom<br />Email: <a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="ku.ca.mac@52cfp" class="oemail">ku.ca.mac@52cfp</a></p><p>Bruce Cohen (<a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="gro.snerdlihcnorka@nehocb" class="oemail">gro.snerdlihcnorka@nehocb</a>) and Patrick Chinnery (<a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="ku.ca.mac@52cfp" class="oemail">ku.ca.mac@52cfp</a>) are actively involved in clinical research regarding individuals with <i>POLG</i>-related disorders. They would be happy to communicate with persons who have any questions regarding diagnosis of <i>POLG</i>-related disorders or other considerations.</p><p>Bruce Cohen (<a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="gro.snerdlihcnorka@nehocb" class="oemail">gro.snerdlihcnorka@nehocb</a>), Patrick Chinnery (<a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="ku.ca.mac@52cfp" class="oemail">ku.ca.mac@52cfp</a>), and Bill Copeland (<a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="vog.hin.shein@1nalepoc" class="oemail">vog.hin.shein@1nalepoc</a>) are also interested in hearing from clinicians treating families affected by mitochondrial disorders in whom no causative variant has been identified through <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> of the genes known to be involved in this group of disorders.</p><p>Contact Dr Bill Copeland (<a href="mailto:dev@null" data-email="vog.hin.shein@1nalepoc" class="oemail">vog.hin.shein@1nalepoc</a>) to inquire about review of <i>POLG</i> variants of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/uncertain-significance/">uncertain significance</a>.</p><p>The Mitochondrial Medicine Society (MMS) represents an international group of physicians, researchers, and clinicians working toward advancing education, research, and global collaboration in clinical mitochondrial medicine. Information about the MMS and educational resources can be found at <a href="https://www.mitosoc.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">www.mitosoc.org</a>.</p></div><div id="alpers.Acknowledgments"><h3>Acknowledgments</h3><p>Funding: This work was funded in part by the Intramural Research Program of the NIEHS, National Institutes of Health [Z01ES065078 and Z01ES065080] to W.C.C.</p><p>P.F.C. is currently funded by a Wellcome Discovery Award (226653/Z/22/Z), a Wellcome Collaborative Award (224486/Z/21/Z), the Medical Research Council Mitochondrial Biology Unit (MC_UU_00028/7), the Biological and Biotechnology Research Council (BB/Y003209/1), and the LifeArc Centre to Treat Mitochondrial Diseases (LAC-TreatMito). His research is supported by the NIHR Cambridge Biomedical Research Centre (BRC-1215-20014). The views expressed are those of the author(s) and not necessarily those of the NIHR or the Department of Health and Social Care.</p></div><div id="alpers.Revision_History"><h3>Revision History</h3><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>29 February 2024 (gm) Comprehensive update posted live</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>1 March 2018 (sw) Comprehensive update posted live</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>18 December 2014 (me) Comprehensive update posted live</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>11 October 2012 (me) Comprehensive update posted live</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>16 March 2010 (me) Review posted live</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>9 December 2007 (bhc) Original submission</div></li></ul></div></div><div id="alpers.References"><h2 id="_alpers_References_">References</h2><div id="alpers.Literature_Cited"><h3>Literature Cited</h3><ul class="simple-list"><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="bk_ref" id="alpers.REF.alves.2018.219">Alves

<script type="text/javascript">
    (function($){
        $('.skiplink').each(function(i, item){
            var href = $($(item).attr('href'));
            href.attr('tabindex', '-1').addClass('skiptarget'); // ensure the target can receive focus
            $(item).on('click', function(event){
                event.preventDefault();
                $.scrollTo(href, 0, {
                    onAfter: function(){
                        href.focus();
                    }
                });
            });
        });
    })(jQuery);
</script>
